### 2026-07-03 (daytime, Claude Code) — shipped 2 HIGH items to main: topshot-sales-history-backfill 429-resilience (Item 1) + Bug 13 restored the moment-page LISTED field off cached_listings_v2. tsc clean (changed files); no DB change

Direct-to-main. Both are code-only. Follows the earlier 07-03 Cowork queue cleanup (32 phantom→empty / 13 frozen→pending) above.

- **Item 1 (topshot-sales-history-backfill 429 storm).** The pipeline was hard rate-limited on ~every TS GQL call — ticks completed in ~0s draining nothing while ~5.6K editions sat pending. Fix spans two files:
  - `lib/chains/flow/topshot.ts` — `topshotGraphql` gained an **opt-in** 3rd arg `TopshotGraphqlOptions { retryOn429, maxRetries, maxBackoffMs }`. When `retryOn429` is set, a 429 is retried honoring the server `Retry-After` header (delta-seconds OR HTTP-date), falling back to exponential backoff (2s·2^n) + jitter, each sleep capped by `maxBackoffMs`. **Default OFF → every existing caller (sniper-feed, badge-sync, …) is byte-identical; zero blast radius.**
  - `app/api/cron/topshot-sales-history-backfill/route.ts` — opts in via `GQL_OPTS { retryOn429, maxRetries:2, maxBackoffMs:8000 }` on both GQL call sites; adds a `GQL_THROTTLE_MS=250` sleep before every set-map/tx page fetch; adds an `INTER_EDITION_DELAY_MS=350` sleep between editions; drops `EDITIONS_PER_TICK` 120→15 (small batch — hundreds/tick just re-trips the limiter); and adds attempt-counter integrity — an edition that exhausts `MAX_EDITION_ATTEMPTS` on a retryable (gql/setmap) error is now counted (`editions_maxed_out`, surfaced in the `pipeline_runs.extra` + a `console.log`) instead of silently freezing in `error` status. Bounded: worst-case retry adds ~16s to one call, well inside `maxDuration=300` with the between-edition budget break. **Verify:** watch `pipeline_runs WHERE pipeline='topshot-sales-history-backfill'` — runs should show `sales_inserted>0` / declining `pending_remaining` once the queue clears. **Revert:** `git revert` the two-file commit (DB untouched; the opt-in param is inert for all other callers).
  - Note: the route calls `public-api.nbatopshot.com` directly (via `topshotGraphql`), not the proxy — 429 (not 403) confirms the requests reach the API; the fix is rate-pacing, not a routing change.
  - **Follow-up (same day, `921f5fc`): `EDITIONS_PER_TICK` 15→40.** A post-deploy `workflow_dispatch` tick verified the fix live — 15 editions ran with only **2 `gql_errors`** (~13% vs the ~83% pre-fix; 13/15 drained, 0 maxed_out, budget NOT hit). Since the batch cap (not the limiter) was bounding throughput on the finite ~5.6K queue, raised to 40 so the 240s `ELAPSED_BUDGET_MS` is the limiter again (throttle + inter-edition delay govern API pressure per call regardless of batch), draining ~2x faster at the same low error rate. Deliberately exceeds the prompt's "5-10/run" (that was a proxy for "don't burst," now enforced by the throttle). **Revert:** set `EDITIONS_PER_TICK` back to 15 (or `git revert 921f5fc`).
- **Item 2 (Bug 13 — restore moment-page LISTED field).** The earlier 07-03 entry HID the field because its only source (`ts_listings`) is dead. This restores it from `cached_listings_v2` — the live on-chain listing feed (currently 21,999 active AllDay listings / 20,900 flow_ids; Golazos 0 right now; no TS rows yet). `app/moment/[id]/page.tsx`: new `fetchActiveListingAsk(nftId)` does `DISTINCT ON`-equivalent (`.eq(flow_id) .is(completed_at,null) .order(price_usd asc) .limit(1)`) → cheapest active ask; wired into the serial-block `Promise.all` (kind='moment' only, keyed on `ss.nft_id`); renders a single `Listed` StatCell (`fmtUsd`, dash when unlisted). Collection-agnostic: TS moments have no rows → honest `—` (not a wrong value off a dead feed); AllDay moments light up (verified `/moment/10658549` → flow_id 10658549 has an active $99 ask). Read-only against `cached_listings_v2`. **Revert:** `git revert` the moment-page commit.

- **SHIPPED — NEXTJS-1H smoke fix (`a80256c`).** The `edition page has Recent Sales` check false-failed daily since 2026-06-08 (HTTP 200 / `Recent Sales=false`). Two compounding causes: (1) it probed `124:4493`, a TS Base-Set common whose bottom sections stream behind `<Suspense>` — on that many-parallel/many-pack page the streamed body blew the 25s fetch budget so the section string was never flushed into the captured body; (2) today's `5370bb0` renamed the section **Recent Sales → Activity**, so the old needle can no longer match on any edition. Retargeted to a light, liquid AllDay Base edition (`/nfl-all-day/edition/446` = Tom Brady — no `::subedition` parallels / special-serials, Suspense block flushes well inside budget) and assert **"Activity"** (the `<Section title="Activity">` heading, rendered unconditionally as the first bottom section; verified it's the only HTML source of that string so the test still proves the module mounted). **Revert:** `git revert a80256c`.
- **SHIPPED — Q6 evm-429 deferral (route commit; Trevor green-lit).** Q6's exponential backoff already existed (shipped 2026-05-31: `2s·2^attempt`+jitter ×4, window-halving, 10k→5k) and the pipeline is ~99% green — so **this is NOT a "no backoff" fix.** The sole residual (rare `ok=false` from `getLogs` exhausting its 4 attempts on a sustained Base burst; 2 in 14d) is now recorded as a **deferral** (`ok=true`, `extra.deferred_rate_limited=true`, `extra.rate_limit_detail`) in `runContract`'s catch, since every 429-prone call precedes any write → cursor never advanced → next tick retries the same window (5k >> Base ~1,800 blk/hr, no lag, no data loss). Non-429 errors still hard-fail. Bumping attempt count past 4 was rejected (exp sleeps would blow `BUDGET_MS=25s`/`maxDuration=60`). **Monitor note (supersedes the standing "evm-429 benign" carry):** these ticks now show `ok=true` + `deferred_rate_limited` instead of `ok=false`; the "pipeline fails 24h" count drops accordingly; count deferrals via `extra->>'deferred_rate_limited'`. Verifiable only by construction + next prod burst (Base WAF blocks non-proxy egress from a dev session). Finding+detail: [docs/handoff-2026-07-03-q6-evm-backoff.md](../handoff-2026-07-03-q6-evm-backoff.md). **Revert:** `git revert` the route commit.

### 2026-07-02 (daytime, Claude Code) — P8 WRITER GUARD SHIPPED: found + fixed the 5th moments-writer (the hydrator's ambiguous (set,play)→edition map); leak stopped, drain now durable

The updated handoff flagged that P8's corrupt-moments set is NOT finite — ~325/24h are actively created by an unidentified 5th moments-writer, so firing the `?p8=1` drain once just re-accumulates. Found the writer, root-caused it, and guarded the write chokepoint. 1 commit (`1cd46de`) + 1 migration (applied via MCP `audit_20260702_replace_topshot_moments_batch_parallel_guard`, repo-synced `supabase/migrations/20260702150000_*`); `check_public_security_invariants()` **[]**; verified live end-to-end.

- **The 5th writer = `topshot-moments-hydrator`** (worker, every 10 min, ~265 moments/run). Its `resolveEditions()` (workers/topshot-moments-hydrator/index.ts L289-298) selects editions by `(set_id_onchain, play_id_onchain)` and builds a `${set}:${play}`→edition uuid map — but the **2026-06-20 subedition catalog cloned those on-chain ids onto every `::` parallel**, so that pair is non-unique (base + all parallels share it) and the map is **overwritten by whichever row PostgREST returns last**. A small-circ `::` parallel can win → a Standard moment lands on it (serial > parallel circ = impossible). NOT the `/api/ingest` `buildEditionKey` (that's submap-gated + 97% of corrupt nfts aren't in the submap), NOT a circulation backfill.
- **Evidence (re-measured live):** 464 corrupt; **328 moment-side inserts/24h, edition-side untouched (0)**, `created_at==updated_at` (fresh inserts of new nfts); the SALES for these nfts sit on **base** (`sale_on_same_parallel=0` all sources) = moment-only mis-key; **452/464 not in `topshot_moment_subeditions`**; **114/114 corrupt `::`editions share (set,play) with a base sibling**; hydrator runs time-correlate to the newest corrupt insert (21:02Z).
- **Fix (root-cause, at the write chokepoint) — `replace_topshot_moments_batch(jsonb)`.** This SECDEF RPC is where every moments writer actually lands rows, so the guard lives here (mirrors the sales-indexer Step-4e + offer_fill base-redirect). A `redirected` CTE, applied **before** the `(edition_id,serial_number)` dedupe (so post-redirect collisions collapse instead of tripping `ON CONFLICT ... cannot affect row a second time`), rewrites any incoming moment on a `::`parallel with `serial > that parallel's circulation` to the **base** `setID:playID` edition; genuine subedition moments (serial ≤ circ) pass through untouched. Catches the hydrator AND any future caller.
- **Deliberately did NOT force base in the hydrator worker.** The hydrator can't place genuine parallels (no submap); forcing all (set,play) resolutions to base would re-collide genuine parallel serials with base serials — the exact conflation the `::`-split solved. The DB guard only touches the unambiguously-impossible case (serial > parallel circ), leaving genuine-parallel placement to the existing submap-driven `remap_topshot_base_keyed_parallel_sales()`.
- **Verified live (MCP):** (1) end-to-end RPC probe — `replace_topshot_moments_batch` with a Standard serial 11 keyed onto `222:7443::20` (circ 10) → **landed on base `222:7443`** (test row cleaned up, no real moment touched); (2) negative replay — **0/500** existing genuine parallels would be redirected; (3) **two** post-guard hydrator cycles (**21:12Z + 21:22Z**, ~268 moments each) → **0 new corrupt** (newest corrupt still 21:02Z, the last pre-guard run; vs the pre-guard ~2–3/run); (4) `check_public_security_invariants()` **[]**.
- **Consequence: `?p8=1` (24fec11) is now a durable one-shot.** New impossible mis-keys are blocked, so the existing 464 are a finite set the drain cleans on firing (operator/deployed, proxy creds). The 175 colliders still need on-chain truth (the drain's `remap_topshot_from_onchain_map()` free-slot leg handles them); the 281 non-colliders could alternatively be bulk-redirected to base via the same guard logic if the operator prefers not to fire the drain. **Revert:** restore the prior function body from `supabase/migrations/20260517131500_replace_topshot_moments_batch_on_conflict_do_update.sql` (+ `git revert 1cd46de`).

### 2026-07-02 (daytime, Claude Code) — P8 MACHINERY SHIPPED (Trevor chose "build the P8 machinery"): on-chain drain mode for the 454 F1-corrupt source `moments`, reusing the proven 06-21 resolver/map/remap

Built the resolution scaffold for P8 (the last non-gated open item in [docs/handoff-2026-07-02-claude-code-remaining.md]): 456 TS `moments` carry `edition_id` → a `::`parallel while `serial > that parallel's circulation` (impossible; F1 parallel mis-attribution inherited into the source moment — what the P7 offer_fill guard reads around). Bad-SALES harm is already blocked by the 4 writer guards + the `topshot_impossible_parallel_serials` sentinel; this closes the deeper source-data hygiene. NOT a clean table re-key — **175 of 456 collide at `UNIQUE(edition_id,serial_number)`**, so the true edition must come from on-chain `getMintedMoment`, not inference. 1 commit (`24fec11`) + 1 migration; `check_public_security_invariants()` **[]** / secdef anon **[]**; route tsc clean; deploy `dpl_9XnCVuauMj59kSyCj7ayprY87zyH` (SHA 24fec11).

- **Discovery — the machinery mostly already existed.** The 06-21 drain (`/api/admin/drain-topshot-misattribution`) already resolves nfts via aliased `getMintedMoment` through topshot-proxy, writes `topshot_misattrib_onchain_map`, and calls `remap_topshot_from_onchain_map()` — which re-keys **both sales AND moments** from the full map, and whose moments leg is already **free-slot-safe** (it defers any target that would collide at `(edition_id,serial)` into `moments_deferred_conflict`, never guessing). It's parameterized by mode (`?wmc=1` swaps the target pool). So P8 needed only a P8 target pool + a mode flag — no new route, no new remap.
- **Migration `audit_20260702_topshot_p8_corrupt_moment_targets`** — new SECDEF `topshot_p8_corrupt_moment_targets(p_limit)` returns the corrupt moments' `nft_id`s (the P8 detector: `::`edition + `circulation_count>0` + `serial>circ`) not yet in `topshot_misattrib_onchain_map`. `SET statement_timeout '120s'`, `search_path public`, service_role-only (anon/authenticated REVOKEd). Verified returns **454** (= 456 − 2 already mapped); invariants/secdef-anon both `[]`.
- **Route `?p8=1` mode (`24fec11`)** — mirrors the existing `?wmc=1` pattern: swaps `targetsRpc` → `topshot_p8_corrupt_moment_targets`, `pipelineName` → `topshot-p8-moment-drain`, and reuses the DEFAULT re-key leg `remap_topshot_from_onchain_map()` (which handles moments). `wmc` takes precedence if both flags set. Purely additive — zero change to the default drain path or any writer.
- **Why on-chain dissolves the 175 collisions:** the collision is an artifact of *inference* (assuming base edition + the same serial). On-chain, each moment resolves to its own true `(set,play,serial)`, so the free-slot check runs against real per-moment targets — most "collisions" evaporate; any genuine residual is honestly reported as `moments_deferred_conflict` for a follow-up look, never guessed.
- **FIRE (deployed, proxy creds auto-injected — CANNOT run from an MCP/Cowork session, topshot-proxy egress):** `GET /api/admin/drain-topshot-misattribution?p8=1&probe=1` (shape-check, no writes) → then `?p8=1&rekey=1` (resolve + re-key). Auth: `Bearer CRON_SECRET` (Vercel cron waits the full 300s) OR `Bearer RPC_ADMIN_TOKEN`/`INGEST_SECRET_TOKEN`, or `?token=<RPC_ADMIN_TOKEN>`. One 300s run covers all 454. Not wired to a recurring cron on purpose — it's a finite backfill (would just no-op once drained). **Verify after:** the P8 detector (corrupt_moments) and `collide_needs_onchain` both fall toward 0; check the run's `moments_deferred_conflict` for any genuine residual.
- **Revert:** `git revert 24fec11` (route mode) + `DROP FUNCTION public.topshot_p8_corrupt_moment_targets(integer);` (target selector). The re-key itself, once fired, is reversible via `audit_topshot_moment_drain_remap_20260621` / `audit_topshot_sale_drain_remap_20260621` (the existing 06-21 audit tables `remap_topshot_from_onchain_map()` writes before-state into).

### 2026-07-02 (daytime, Claude Code) — P1b RESIDUAL CLOSED (Trevor: "do what you think is best long term for RPC's data integrity"): broadened the disconnected-ASK clamp to the actively-traded bimodal class + made it lag-proof by folding it into fmv-recalc

Closed the residual the prior P1b ship (`ed10bb9`) left open (documented in the handoff): the model clamp fixed FMVs disconnected ABOVE their max sale (73 editions) but missed the bimodal class (low median + a stale high outlier that sits BELOW the edition's own max — e.g. Derrick White `218:8204` still $23.80 live vs median $0.29 / p90 $0.95). Root-caused as a **gate gap + a timing lag**, not a logic bug. 1 migration + 1 route change (`78501ba`); `check_public_security_invariants()` **[]**; tsc clean (fmv-recalc); deploy `dpl_BA4kMwTYd8AiUADdcLrdErSptUhu` (SHA 78501ba).

- **Gate broadened — migration `fmv_clamp_disconnected_ask_topshot_broaden_bimodal_gate`.** Replaced the tiered gate `((circ≥1000 & fmv>3×p90) OR fmv>8×p90)` with `fmv > 3×median AND fmv > 1.5×p90` (over >$0.10 90d sales). This is **strictly broader** — since p90≥median, both old legs are subsumed (fmv>8×p90 ⟹ fmv>8×med>3×med; fmv>3×p90 likewise) — so it catches everything the old gate did plus the mid-band. Clamp target unchanged `GREATEST(p90×1.5, median)`: the p90×1.5 headroom **spares genuine wide editions** (Edwards `218:7908` $7.42 < 1.5×$7.20 → untouched) and lands rare low-circ parallels **near their realized high** not their median (Vassell `245:8440::20` circ 10 → $69.30 vs $75 max). LOW/ASK_ONLY only; **0 HIGH/MEDIUM touched.** Also guarded the `pipeline_runs` INSERT behind `IF v_clamped>0` so the new inline caller doesn't flood the table with 0-row rows. **Ran live: 217 clamped, $473.90 removed** (74 old-gate + 143 mid-band). Spot: Derrick White `218:8204` $23.80→$1.43, Vince Williams `218:8240` $7→$1.50, Embiid `26:745` $21.25→$15.75 (real p90 $10.50), Vassell $179.10→$69.30. **Revert:** `CREATE OR REPLACE` the function back to the circ/8×p90 gate (prior def in Supabase migration history).
- **Lag killed — Step 10 inline in `app/api/fmv-recalc/route.ts`.** The clamp cron `rpc-fmv-clamp-disconnected-ask` fires **13:55Z**, but the big recalc sweeps run **~15:00Z + ~22:00Z** and rewrote fresh unclamped `_haircut` snapshots AFTER it — so the latest snapshot was the inflated fake for ~23h/day (the clamp was structurally a day behind). Fixed by calling the clamp **inline as Step 10** (right after the Step 8 haircut / Step 9 thin-sales guard — same inline-DB-guard pattern those already use), gated to runs that wrote Top Shot editions (`sawTopShot`). Every recalc now ends clamped → no lag. Non-fatal (a clamp error never fails the recalc); surfaced in `pipeline_runs.extra.disconnected_ask_clamp_rows`. Daily cron stays as the backstop. **Revert:** delete the Step 10 block (`git revert 78501ba`).
- **Verification:** invariants `[]`; `fmv_sanity_flags` 0; detector `fmv>3×med AND fmv>1.5×p90` (n≥5) 162→**32**, and all 32 are non-clampable — 11 MEDIUM (confident sales-based, excluded by design) + 21 already-clamped sitting exactly at their rounded target (`ROUND(p90×1.5,2)` lands a hair above the detector's unrounded `1.5×p90`); **0 genuinely-unclamped fakes.** The handoff's P1b residual note is now resolved. **P1b is fully closed** — the model clamp catches both the disconnected + bimodal classes, is born-clamped every recalc, and has a daily-cron + display-guard belt-and-suspenders.

### 2026-07-02 (daytime, Claude Code) — P1b SHIPPED (Trevor "do everything… best long-term approach"): FMV model root-cause clamp for the bimodal fake-deal class the P1a max-sale guard can't reach

Root-caused P1b and shipped the durable model fix + a read-side safety net. **Key correction to the handoff's diagnosis:** the worst fakes are NOT the WAP over-weighting outliers — they carry algo_version `_haircut`/`cold-tail-1.0`. `fmv_apply_thin_sale_haircut` decays an ASK-pinned FMV once (×0.55–0.85) then STICKS (its `fmv≈floor` re-fire condition breaks), and the cold-tail path prices cold editions off a stale/troll ASK — so `51:1952` shows FMV $42.50 with a 90d **max sale of $0.33**, and Giannis `8:62` shows **$2,924 ASK_ONLY** vs a $2 p90. All disconnected-high FMVs are LOW/ASK_ONLY. 2 migrations + 1 pg_cron + 1 lib file (`ed10bb9`); `check_public_security_invariants()` **[]** / secdef anon **[]**; tsc clean; deploy `dpl_DYCAC…` (READY-pending at write).

- **P1b MODEL root fix — `audit_20260702_fmv_clamp_disconnected_ask_topshot` (`fmv_clamp_disconnected_ask_topshot()` + pg_cron `rpc-fmv-clamp-disconnected-ask` jobid 34 @ `55 13`).** Snaps LOW/ASK_ONLY TS FMVs disconnected from active trading down to `GREATEST(p90×1.5, median)` of non-gift (>$0.10) 90d sales — p90 is the honest anchor (robust to the lone outlier that pollutes `max`, which is exactly why P1a's clamp-to-max misses this class: their FMV sits BELOW that outlier). **Tiered gate proven safe by live measurement:** clamp when a high-circ common (circ≥1000) has fmv > 3× p90, OR ANY edition has fmv > 8× p90 (troll asks). HIGH/MEDIUM (confident sales-based) prices NEVER touched; low-pop grails (ask ≤~4× p90, e.g. `238:8028::20` $899 vs p90 $537 = 1.7×) NEVER touched; genuine bimodal editions with a real upper mode (`218:7908` $7.42 ≈ p90 $7.20) correctly spared. **Ran once now: 107 editions clamped, $3,472.67 removed, 0 legit grails, 0 HIGH/MED, idempotent (re-run=0), fmv_sanity_flags stays 0.** Spot: `8:62` $2924→$3.00, `171:6424` $170→$0.68, `51:1952` $42.50→$0.49, Derrick White `218:8204` $23.80→$1.43. Clamped rows tagged algo_version `…_p90clamp`. Corrects `fmv_snapshots` itself → fixes deal boards, alerts, pack-EV, concierge, FMV API, Market, Sniper in ONE place. **Revert:** `DROP FUNCTION public.fmv_clamp_disconnected_ask_topshot(boolean); SELECT cron.unschedule('rpc-fmv-clamp-disconnected-ask');` (clamped rows reprice from sales on the next fmv-recalc sweep — the prior inflated value is not stored).
- **P1b READ-SIDE safety net — `audit_20260702_fmv_display_guard_p90_disconnected`.** `topshot_fmv_display_guard` gains `p90_90d`/`fmv_disconnected`/`clamp_target`; `refresh_topshot_fmv_display_guard()` computes the same tiered rule; `lib/fmv-display-guard.ts` clamps effective FMV to `clamp_target` at request time. Driven by an INDEPENDENT daily cron (jobid 33) so if the model-clamp cron ever pauses (cf. the special-serial-MV history), the hero boards stay honest. Currently 0 rows flagged (the model already cleaned the source) — a dormant net that activates only on future re-inflation. **Revert:** restore the prior `refresh_topshot_fmv_display_guard()` body (git) + `ALTER TABLE public.topshot_fmv_display_guard DROP COLUMN p90_90d, DROP COLUMN fmv_disconnected, DROP COLUMN clamp_target;` + `git revert ed10bb9` for the lib.
- **Durability model:** daily pg_cron re-clamps any editions a recalc re-inflates; the request-time display guard closes the intraday window for Market/Sniper. Follow-up (measured, not built): extend the same clamp to AllDay/Golazos/UFC once their disconnected-ASK populations are measured (thresholds are TS-calibrated). **P1b is now SHIPPED — remove from the review-gated queue.**

New `BEFORE INSERT` trigger `trg_zzz_allday_cross_source_dedup` (fn `allday_sales_cross_source_dedup`, SECDEF, anon/authenticated REVOKEd) uses the sweeper's exact economic key (`nft_id + round(price,2) + day`) restricted to a DIFFERENT source; on a match it merges the field-wise best buyer/seller/serial into the surviving twin and skips the incoming insert (keep-richer, **no in-trigger DELETE**, INSERT-only so no recursion). Both prior objections addressed: (1) keep-richer not keep-first — downstream data is identical to the sweeper result, only the surviving row's immaterial source label differs; (2) **short-circuits on `collection_id` before any query, so only AllDay inserts (~337/day) pay the lookup — the hot TS write path is untouched.** Hourly sweeper stays as the backstop for intra-batch races. **Verified:** 5-scenario self-rolling-back test (cross-source collapse+union / same-source kept / diff-price kept / non-AllDay TS kept) + a live committed-trigger test (2 cross-source AllDay inserts → 1 row, unioned buyer/seller/serial=9, rolled back zero-residue); trigger propagated to all `sales_YYYY` partitions; invariants []. Detector (`dup_groups` cross-source) currently **0** (sweeper already clean; trigger is preventive). **Revert:** `DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS trg_zzz_allday_cross_source_dedup ON public.sales; DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS public.allday_sales_cross_source_dedup();` (+ `git revert 6b7cda1` for the repo migration file). The F2/P2 "deferred / cadence-bump-only" entries below are now superseded by this ship.

- **F1 — sales-indexer resolver gap CLOSED (`8064801`, [app/api/sales-indexer/route.ts]).** The a9c011c *writer* fix stopped `/api/ingest` keying `::` from GQL, but the sales-indexer's own *resolution* could still land a Standard nft on a `::subID` parallel: a stale `moments`/`wmc` row keyed to the parallel edition passes `isCanonicalExtId` (canonical FORMAT) and Steps 4a/4b trust it. Proven live: nft `52062637` is in `moments` keyed to `257:8664::18` (circ **50**) with **serial 551** — impossible for a /50 parallel; it's a Standard (`257:8664`, circ 1149) mis-keyed. The Step 4d GQL fallback already resolves everything to base only, so a `::` assignment only ever originates from a pre-existing 4a/4b row. **Fix:** new Step 4e loads confirmed genuine parallels from `topshot_moment_subeditions` (`subedition_id > 0`) and, per event, redirects any `::subID` resolution to the base `setID:playID` edition unless the submap confirms the nft is a genuine parallel — making 4a/4b consistent with 4d. Measured: keeps **11,893** confirmed parallels, redirects **682** unconfirmed Standard-on-parallel mis-keys (674 not-in-submap + 8 submap-contradicted). Adds `parallel_redirects` telemetry. Closes the resolver trickle so CC's daily self-healer no longer has to chase it. **Revert:** `git revert 8064801`.
- **F4 — reward-pack dead-counter KPIs suppressed (`c5db7a4`, pack dist page).** Reward/quest packs (retail=0) mint-on-demand → `pack_distributions.total_minted / total_opened / total_pack_count` are dead-by-design (`packs_opened` ~6× "minted"; dist 7800: 21,387 opened vs 3,240 minted — see [[pack-ev-view-dataquality-footguns]]). The top-grid **Depletion** + **Packs remaining / of N minted** KPIs and the `PacksContentRemaining` **donut ring** read those dead counters and contradict the honest Observed pack lifecycle strip beside them. **Fix:** hide the two KPI tiles for `isRewardPack` and null the donut ring's `unopened`/`totalMinted` inputs (the live-pool per-tier bars use real pool data and stay). 6 affected TS reward dists. **Revert:** `git revert c5db7a4`.
- **F2 — cross-source dedup at ingest: ANALYZED, deliberately NOT shipped (steady-state-covered, risky hot-path writer change).** The validated `dedup_allday_cross_source_sales()` sweeper (jobid 32, `40 12 * * *`) keys on `(nft_id, round(price,2), day)` cross-source only AND **keeps the richer row** (max buyer+seller+serial), merging losers. A "skip-if-exists" BEFORE INSERT trigger on the year-partitioned `sales` hot path would instead keep whichever row arrives first — often the poorer `allday_studio_history_v1` row over a richer forward `onchain_dapper_v2` row → *degrades* data quality vs the sweeper — plus adds a per-row EXISTS lookup to the hottest write path. A one-directional "studio defers to onchain" check covers only one arrival order. Current backlog **25 dup groups / 25 victims** (~0.08% of 30,352 90d AllDay sales), collapsed daily. Conclusion: the trickle is negligible and steady-state-covered; a correct keep-richer ingest dedup is a non-trivial hot-path design that isn't one-pass-safe/verifiable. Left to the daily sweep; documented the design constraint (keep-richer) for any future dedicated pass. Not worth the risk/benefit now.

### 2026-06-30 (Cowork) — serial-fit lookback tuned per-bucket (jersey→365d) + `statement_timeout`→600s (resolves SERIAL-FMV-POWER-MODEL-WEEKLY-TIMEOUT); pack-EV honesty + explicit-circ MEASURED→settled

**SHIPPED — `audit_20260630_serial_fmv_fits_statement_timeout_600s` + per-bucket fit-window tuning (Trevor-approved).** Asked whether deeper/longer sales history improves FMV context: NO backfill needed — TS sales already reach the 2020 launch (~5.9y). The soft FMV tail is LIQUIDITY not coverage (of 12,748 non-HIGH/MED editions: 44.5% traded <90d = honest wide-spread LOW, 51.6% NEVER traded = nothing to backfill, ~4% stale-but-already-held). The one real lever = a longer FIT WINDOW for the thin serial buckets. Measured per-bucket log-log fit stability at 180/365/730/all-time: **jersey clearly better at 365d** (r 0.689→0.702, beta stable), **first indifferent** (200+ samples already ample for a 2-param fit), **perfect worse at 365d** (older higher perfect sales pull k up -> +24% median overshoot vs +15% at 180d); every bucket degrades past 1yr (5y price drift; all-time r < 180d). So tuned per-bucket rather than blanket-365.

- **Final config (cron-only via `cron.alter_job`, NO fn-body rewrite — the lookback is a fn param):** job 30 `rpc-serial-fmv-jersey-weekly` -> `SELECT public.compute_serial_fmv_jersey_model(p_lookback_days => 365)` (jersey ALL n 358->459, r 0.690->0.702; calibration pred $48.66 vs actual $50, APE 47.3%). The migration `ALTER FUNCTION`s all 3 fit fns `SET statement_timeout='600s'` (kept regardless of window) -> **resolves the carried SERIAL-FMV-POWER-MODEL-WEEKLY-TIMEOUT** (jobid 6 had been failing weekly at the 120s session cap).

Broad-market sales validation still holds (OK mid-market, underprices grails). Refinement directions in the handoff. Do NOT wire until re-gated.
- **Jersey model REFIT v2 (`audit_20260630_jersey_model_refit_beta_below_1`, read-only).** Sales-residual analysis (the real arbiter): jersey premium multiple is inverse-to-base-FMV (~10.6× base<$50 n=150 → ~2× base≥$150 n=11), and 0 jersey sales exist above the fit range. The v1 RARE β=1.09 was an overfit over-extrapolating grails. Tightened reliability β<1.0 → only COMMON+ALL-pooled reliable (sub-linear); RARE/LEG/FANDOM→pooled. v2: base<$150 (96% of volume) calibrated 0.88–0.94 / APE ~0.50; grails conservative (≈base above ~$450). vs LT v2 is below LT on grails BUT sales back v2 → LT over-prices illiquid grails (don't chase). Net: jersey is sales-validated/wireable for the bulk with a high-FMV low-confidence flag; grail tail unmodelable (thin data). Still INERT. Revert: re-create the fit fn with β<1.25 + recompute.
- **Deep multi-factor serial analysis (2026-06-30, read-only).** Tested "value from many property factors" with 5-fold CV (held-out $APE) across #1/perfect/jersey (n=679/242/186, FMV-anchored). Only FMV+tier+circ generalize for #1 (testR² 0.66 / APE 44%); perfect=FMV (0.54/46%); jersey=FMV (0.34/52%). `series`, `play_category`, AND `player-stardom` all OVERFIT (raise train fit, not held-out; player-star redundant with edition FMV). Noise floor ~44–52% APE is irreducible (special-serial prices high-variance). A many-factor model = fitting noise; the parsimonious FMV+tier+circ model is the honest ceiling, and the existing power-law models are near-optimal (one validated gain: explicit circ for #1). Detail in handoff "Deep multi-factor analysis".
- **Serial factors — rookie/TS-Debut badges + player career scarcity also REJECTED (2026-06-30).** CV-tested Trevor's intuitive factors on #1 (n=336) from `badge_editions` (Top Shot Debut, Rookie Year/Premiere/RoY, badge_score) + per-player Σcirc (Wemby-scarcity): base FMV+tier+circ R² 0.536 → +each ~flat (0.533–0.536), +ALL four 0.528 (overfit); coeffs ~0/wrong-signed conditional on FMV. WHY: edition FMV is a near-sufficient statistic — the market already prices rookie/debut/scarcity INTO base FMV, so they're redundant in the serial-premium layer (they belong in the base FMV model). Confirms: serial premium = f(FMV,tier,circ) by type; nothing else generalizes.
- **Special-serial trends + PARALLELS swept (2026-06-30, descriptive).** #1 multiple is monotone-inverse to circ (mass-common 35× / $25 → grail 3.8×) + tier (COMMON 18× / LEG 2.5×); vintage abs-premium (S1/S2 $1k–9k, thin); set high-mult = cheap-common sets (Extra Spice 42×); player superstar cheap-common #1s 28–50× (Luka 50/Steph 49/Wemby 28/LeBron 15) vs pumped-rookie low-mult. PARALLELS: 137 #1+126 perfect/yr; multiple tracks parallel scarcity EXACTLY (Club Collection circ99 7.8× → Galactic circ5 1.4×); base-#1 cheaper when parallels exist (9.6× vs 5.9×, no parallel-exists discount). Parallels need NO separate model — each `::` is its own edition with own FMV+circ, so FMV+tier+circ prices them right; only lever = per-parallel circ/FMV accuracy (~22% of `::` still floor-circ). Detail: handoff trends+parallels section.
- **Base edition-FMV factor test — badges/series/rookie DO predict base value (2026-06-30, the thin-edition lever).** Mirror of the serial finding: predicting ln(edition FMV) from FEATURES ONLY (no own sales), 5-fold CV n=700: tier+circ R² 0.643/51% → +series 0.681/47% → +badges(TS-Debut/rookie/badge_score) **0.732/43%**. Coeffs: LEG +2.57, ln(circ) −0.556, newer-series negative (vintage premium real), rookie +0.137, badge_score +0.136/pt; player-career-circ weak (−0.025). So property factors are redundant for the serial PREMIUM (already in FMV) but PREDICTIVE for BASE value — usable as a feature PRIOR for NO_DATA/thin-LOW editions (~213 NO_DATA + LOW tail) where sales-recalc has no signal. Review-gated base-FMV pricing change (LT gate + Trevor). Detail: handoff base-FMV section.
- **Feature-prior stress-test + CORRECTION (2026-06-30).** Built `v_topshot_fmv_feature_prior` (read-only/INERT, cohort median FMV tier×series×circ_band×rookie; 39% APE/98% cov held-out). CORRECTION: the cohort prior is PLAYER-BLIND → its over_3x divergence is dominated by LEGIT STARS (Flagg Rookie-Debut $1,876 vs prior $6, LeBron $819, Jokić $8,999), NOT mispricing — so the earlier "323 stale-chase pool editions" read was WRONG. Player-aware prior is worse (80% APE, sparse — most players <3 priced editions). Bottom line: no feature-only substitute for sales on the star tail; cohort prior helps only the NON-STAR thin tail (~31/188 NO_DATA). REINFORCES: edition FMV (sales-derived, stardom-aware) is the near-sufficient statistic; thin-tail lever = more sales density, not a feature prior. View kept as honest cohort reference (comment fixed). Revert: DROP VIEW v_topshot_fmv_feature_prior.

AllDay's huge pack ids (e.g. `222101351039284`) never collide with the integer TS ids, so opens upsert idempotently on `pack_nft_id` with **zero DDL on the shared table** — the one risky part of the handoff was moot.

Resolving dist from `pack_purchases` where present (dist 180) + NULL otherwise, exactly like ~25% of TS rips.

So **opener = modal `AllDay.Deposit.to`**, **moments_pulled = count(`AllDay.Deposit`)**. Flow REST (`rest-mainnet.onflow.org`) reachable from Supabase egress (no proxy) — confirmed.

Writes `pack_rips` (`onConflict pack_nft_id ignoreDuplicates`) + `allday_pack_pull`; resolves dist from `pack_purchases`; self-logs `pipeline_runs` (`allday-pack-opens-{forward,backfill}`). - **VERIFIED end-to-end:** banked **28 AllDay rips / ~87 moments / 87 pulls** in-session (cursor draining from tip). Trevor's open landed exactly (pack `222101351039284`, opener `0xbd94…50ac`, moment `9781818`). **`get_wallet_pack_summary('0xbd94…')` now returns NFL All Day `packs_ripped: 1`** — per-wallet AllDay pack history (summary + `get_wallet_pack_history` timeline) lights up automatically with ZERO app change. `pipeline_runs` logging confirmed ok=true. security invariants `[]`.

### 2026-06-28 (daytime, Claude Code — session 7) — drained the pack-lifecycle-DURABILITY handoff: extended the security invariant to catch the view-hardening regression it missed, closed 9 view write-grant holes, restored the deals-board invoker flag (cleared the lone Supabase ERROR), made the TS pack seeder non-destructive. Corrected 2 wrong handoff premises.

Drained [docs/handoff-2026-06-28-pack-lifecycle-durability.md](../handoff-2026-06-28-pack-lifecycle-durability.md) (Trevor: "do everything outlined, plus remedy anything you find"). 2 DB migrations + 1 DB RPC migration + 1 edge-fn redeploy; `check_public_security_invariants()` returns **0**, Supabase `security_definer_view` ERROR **1→0**. No app code changed (edge fn is Deno; views are MCP-applied per convention).

- **SHIPPED — security invariant extended + drift baseline (`audit_20260628_view_security_invariants_and_definer_drift`).** The handoff (§2) flagged that `check_public_security_invariants()` did NOT catch the EV view silently losing `security_invoker` + keeping `authenticated` writes — because it only scanned BASE TABLES. **Faithful-but-improved fix** (a naive "flag every view without security_invoker" would return 34 mostly-intentional definer views → a standing false BREACH every run): added (a) **`view_updatable_anon_write`** — auto-updatable (`is_insertable_into='YES'`) public views granted anon/auth writes (the genuine RLS-bypass write vectors), and (b) **`view_unexpected_definer`** — definer public views NOT in a new `security_definer_view_allowlist` baseline table (catches a hardened/invoker view reverting to definer, e.g. the next EV-view re-deploy). Seeded the allowlist with the **33** currently-accepted definer views. Invariant verified **0 rows** post-fix. NOTE: there is no repo source file for the EV/lifecycle views (DB-only, MCP-applied), so the handoff's "add WITH (security_invoker=on) to the source" has nothing to edit — the allowlist drift-check is the durable substitute (better: it catches ANY future regression, not just this one view). **Revert:** `CREATE OR REPLACE` the prior 2-branch function body; `DROP TABLE public.security_definer_view_allowlist;`.
- **SHIPPED — closed 9 live view write-grant holes (same migration).** REVOKEd anon/authenticated INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE on the 8 auto-updatable views (`beta_feedback_inbox`, `collection_chains`, `flowty_funded_loans`, `flowty_limbo_loans`, `flowty_open_listings`, `flowty_scanner_health`, `pack_distributions_v`, `ufc_studio_sales_history_backfill_targets`) + `v_topshot_edition_pull_provenance` (inert aggregate, revoked for parity). **Revert:** `GRANT INSERT,UPDATE,DELETE,TRUNCATE ON <view> TO authenticated;` (anon where it had it).
- **SHIPPED — restored `cross_collection_deals_board` security_invoker=on (same migration). FOUND while auditing.** The public deals-board route header ([app/api/public/insights/deals/route.ts](../../app/api/public/insights/deals/route.ts)) documents this view as `security_invoker=on`, but a `CREATE OR REPLACE` had silently dropped it — it was the project's **only** Supabase `security_definer_view` ERROR. All 6 deps anon-selectable + route reads via service_role → flip restores intent, clears the ERROR, breaks nothing. ERROR **1→0** confirmed via advisor. **Revert:** `ALTER VIEW public.cross_collection_deals_board RESET (security_invoker);` (don't — keep the fix).
- **SHIPPED — non-destructive TS pack seeder (`audit_20260628_seed_topshot_pack_distributions_nondestructive` RPC + edge-fn redeploy v20, commit `ba3b010`).** Handoff §1: the `seed-topshot-pack-distributions` `.upsert()` reset `total_minted→0` + REPLACED metadata every re-seed (observed 1,983→474). PostgREST upsert can't express partial-column + metadata-merge, so added SECDEF `seed_topshot_pack_distributions(p_rows jsonb)` (service_role-only — functions are anon/auth-executable by default here, so locked) doing `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` that **never touches total_minted/total_opened** and **merges** `metadata = existing || incoming`; edge fn now calls `.rpc()` (chunk 500) instead of `.upsert()`. **Proven** on a synthetic throwaway dist: `total_minted=999`/`total_opened=42` preserved, metadata merged (kept `backfilled`, updated `a`, added `c`). Final confirmation = next scheduled cron tick (INGEST token blank locally). **Revert:** `git revert <hash>` + redeploy prior edge-fn; `DROP FUNCTION public.seed_topshot_pack_distributions(jsonb);`.
- **CORRECTED handoff §3 — rip_dist cleanup is UNSAFE, NOT done.** The handoff called `DELETE FROM topshot_pack_rip_attribution WHERE method='rip_dist'` "safe" (resolvers only target NULL-dist rows). Measurement disproves it twice: (1) of 39,339 `rip_dist` rows, **184 are load-bearing** (`pack_rips.dist_id IS NULL` → the lifecycle view's COALESCE falls back to the attribution row); (2) more importantly, **`attribute_topshot_rips_empirical` reads the ENTIRE rip_dist set as its empirical training data** (`dist_support`: dists with ≥20 rip_dist rows → `emp_pool` of observed editions). Deleting them would degrade pack attribution. Left intact. Do NOT re-suggest the blanket delete.
- **SHIPPED (Trevor "Proceed") — root fix for the universal `authenticated`/`anon` write grants (`audit_20260628_revoke_default_and_existing_view_writes`).** Root cause: `pg_default_acl` granted `authenticated=arwdDxtm` (full write) on every new relation created by `postgres`, and the `supabase_admin` default even granted `anon` full write. Two-part fix: (1) `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA public REVOKE INSERT,UPDATE,DELETE,TRUNCATE ON TABLES FROM anon, authenticated` — new **postgres-created** objects (= all MCP/app migrations) now get SELECT but NOT write (verified: a throwaway probe view came back anon SELECT/REFERENCES + authenticated SELECT/REFERENCES/TRIGGER, **0 write**); (2) swept **55** existing public views, revoking anon/auth writes (no INSTEAD OF triggers → no view is intentionally writable; writes always go to base tables / service_role RPCs). **SCOPE BOUNDARIES (deliberate):** base TABLES untouched — RLS backstops their write grants and some carry legitimate policy-backed anon-INSERT paths (`email_subscribers`/`outbound_clicks`/`portfolio_snapshots`/`support_conversations`); a blanket table sweep would break those. The `supabase_admin`-grantor default is UNCHANGED (MCP role `postgres` lacks rights to alter it; only dashboard/internal-created objects use it, not app migrations). **FUTURE-MIGRATION IMPLICATION:** a new table needing an authenticated/anon write path must now add an EXPLICIT `GRANT INSERT,... TO authenticated` next to its RLS policy — a forgotten grant is a LOUD permission-denied, not silent data loss. Post-change: invariant **0**, anon SELECT on public boards preserved (deals board still readable). **Revert:** `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA public GRANT INSERT,UPDATE,DELETE,TRUNCATE ON TABLES TO authenticated;` (+ re-grant on the swept views if any path actually needed it — none should).
- **VERIFIED no-action:** `topshot_pack_supply` is RLS-on, anon/auth SELECT-only (handoff §1 claim holds); the EV + 2 lifecycle + provenance views all already carry `security_invoker=on`.

### 2026-06-28 (daytime, Claude Code — session 6) — "proceed with all": shipped Item 3 (repaired the broken gql_historical pool backfill — the attribution lift) + Item 2-calibrate (non-destructive calibrated EV). Both were "queued" in session 5.

Trevor: "Proceed with all" on the session-5 queue. **Handoff was stale on Item 3** — Cowork BUILT + deployed the `backfill-topshot-pack-supply` edge fn (supply + pool modes) on 2026-06-28 *after* writing the 06-27 handoff. So Item 3 became "fix Cowork's pool mode," not "build from scratch."

- **Item 3 — SHIPPED: repaired `backfill-topshot-pack-supply` mode=pool (commit `90ff900` edge-fn source + DB).** Cowork's pool mode had written **0** `gql_historical` rows since deploy — two bugs, both found by a no-write `mode=debugpool` probe I added: **(1)** the single-line `EDITIONS_QUERY`/`_LEGACY` minification **dropped the final closing brace** → every call 422'd `GRAPHQL_PARSE_FAILED` (supply's `DYNAMIC_QUERY` was minified correctly, so supply worked + masked it); **(2)** the `pack_drop_pool` upsert used `onConflict (collection_id,dist_id,edition_id)` but the PK is **4-col incl. `slot_name`** → `no unique/exclusion constraint`. Fixed both (write `slot_name='default'`, the table-wide convention; 4-col onConflict), added V3→legacy auto-fallback + GQL body capture, and moved the paginated work to **background** (`EdgeRuntime.waitUntil` → 202 ack) because a synchronous response 504'd at the ~150s gateway cap. Per-dist upsert is all-or-nothing (accumulate pages → one upsert) so a killed worker leaves no partial pool. **Verified live:** V3 now returns `flowId:82/flowID:2841`; pool rows landing int-keyed (`165:6562`…). **Self-draining:** new pg_cron `rpc-backfill-pack-pool` (`5,15,25,35,45,55 * * * *`, mode=pool limit 25 conc 2, off cron-rush); targets RPC tightened (`audit_20260628_pool_backfill_targets_exclude_any_pool` — exclude ANY-pooled dist, not just gql_historical, so it skips the ~568 EV-path `gql` pools and converges, rips-dists first). **End-to-end proven:** ran `backfill_pack_rip_metadata(2000)` → `dist_resolved 1381`, then `attribute_topshot_rips_empirical(20000)` → coverage ticked up (39,489→39,634 attributed). Full coverage lift past 21% drains over the next hours as the pool cron walks the ~1,400 unpooled dists (rips-first); the hourly `:53 backfill_pack_rip_metadata` + the daily attribution cron pick up new pools automatically. **Revert:** `git revert 90ff900`; redeploy the prior edge-fn (pre-fix); `SELECT cron.unschedule('rpc-backfill-pack-pool');`; restore the prior `get_topshot_pool_backfill_targets` body; `DELETE FROM pack_drop_pool WHERE pool_source='gql_historical';`

Surfaced on the pack reality-check only when it diverges ≥10% from modeled. **Verified:** on-model `6452` 18.61→**18.73** (~unchanged); over-valued `5640` 46.29→**11.37**, `1548` 47.95→**16.54**, `7584` 70.18→**28.56** (pulled toward observed pulls). ### 2026-06-27 (daytime, Claude Code — session 4) — closed the LAST cron-surface SPOF: concurrency guard + GHA backstop for the wallet-backfill / snapshot writer families (the heavy HIGHs Item 4 deferred to an "atomic operator cutover")

Drained the `5244c37` CC prompt ("cron->GHA decouple pt2 — guard-then-backstop"). Item 4's HIGH sales indexers already got a backstop (`cfe034e`); this is the families it correctly left out because they can't be naively double-fired. **Key reframe (correct):** the hazard is NOT dup rows (both families are already row-idempotent — `upsert_wmc_batch ON CONFLICT`, snapshot UPSERT on `(wallet,collection,snapshot_at)`), it's **2× on-chain Cadence per wallet + connection-pool/IO saturation (the 2026-06-10 DBSAT class)**. So: make a concurrent invocation a NO-OP FIRST, then the backstop needs NO atomic cutover. 1 commit (`611b2fb`), 1 migration, 1 edge-fn redeploy, 2 GHA workflows; tsc clean; security 0/0/0/0; deploy READY.

- **DB — `audit_20260627_pipeline_run_locks_concurrency_guard` (service_role only).** New `pipeline_run_locks(lock_key pk, status, claimed_at, released_at, attempts)` (RLS on, anon/auth REVOKEd) + `claim_pipeline_lock(p_key, p_stale_seconds default 660)` / `release_pipeline_lock(p_key)`. **Claim-row pattern (mirrors `allow_list_claim_prewarm`), deliberately NOT an advisory xact lock** — a PostgREST-driven walk spans many separate transactions, so `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` would auto-release after the first call and protect nothing; a durable claim row survives the multi-second walk and across separate lambdas. Atomic `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE … WHERE (status='done' OR claimed_at < now()-stale)` → a fresh in_progress returns false (no-op); a `done`/stale row is reclaimable. Stale=660s is safely **above** the 600s maxDuration ceiling on the heaviest child, so a legitimately-running long walk is never falsely stale, while a hard-killed lambda's lock self-clears in ≤11 min. **Verified live:** claim→true, 2nd claim→false (blocked), release→done, reclaim→true; stale=0 always reclaims; security invariants 0, secdef-anon 0, tables_without_rls 0.
- **Guard helper — [lib/wallet-backfill-lock.ts]** (`claimPipelineLock`/`releasePipelineLock`, key `wallet-backfill:<slug>:<wallet>`). **FAIL-OPEN**: any lock RPC error/throw → claim returns true (proceed), so the guard can NEVER become a new SPOF that halts ingest. Per-(collection,wallet) key so the 5 children for one wallet never block each other (the multicollection orchestrator fans them out concurrently).
- **Family #2 wiring (the expensive one — overlap = 2× Cadence/wallet).** Guard at the top of `runBackfill` (TS, [app/api/wallet-backfill/route.ts]) + the 3 shared runners in [lib/chains/flow/wallet-backfill-helpers.ts] — `runIdOnlyBackfill` (golazos+ufc), `runAllDayDetailsBackfill`, `runPinnacleDetailsBackfill`. A concurrent walk no-ops: fire-and-forget runners log `terminated_reason=skipped_in_progress` + return rowsFound 0; the sync runners (allday/pinnacle) additionally return `complete:true` so the orchestrator stops polling that collection for that wallet. Released in `finally`. `runPaginatedDetailsBackfill` is internal-only (called inside the guarded allday/pinnacle runners) so it's intentionally NOT separately guarded.
- **Family #1 wiring (snapshot — read-only, belt-and-suspenders).** Guard added to the edge fn `runWork` (claim at the `Deno.serve` call site, try/finally release, skip→logs `skipped_in_progress`). **Edge fn redeployed v10→v11** (`verify_jwt=false` preserved).
- **Backstops (redundant, harmless thanks to the guard — NO cutover):** [.github/workflows/wallet-backfill-backstop.yml] fires the 4 `seed-wallet-refresh?cohort=K&of=4` cohorts at `38 2,8,14,20` UTC (GHA-empty minute, ~2h after each primary 6h window, staggered 60s). [.github/workflows/snapshot-institutional-wallets-backstop.yml] fires `/api/cron/snapshot-institutional-wallets` daily `7 7` UTC (~30m after the 06:37 primary). Both Bearer `INGEST_SECRET_TOKEN`, `continue-on-error`, `www.` host (pushed via `gh auth token` — workflow scope).
- **NOT done (deliberate):** the optional per-cohort orchestrator coarse-guard (the per-wallet guard already makes overlap harmless, so it's redundant complexity). cron-job.org primary disable stays operator-only (secret-gated DOM) — dual-trigger is intentional until then.
- **VERIFY (post-deploy watch):** next `38`/`07:07` GHA ticks 200/202; on the next cron×GHA overlap, a 2nd same-wallet walk logs `skipped_in_progress` and on-chain Cadence volume does NOT double (watch `pipeline_run_locks` + the analytics-smoke saturation legs + DBSAT); `detect_stalled_pipelines()` stays `[]`; wmc row counts flat. **Revert:** `git revert 611b2fb`; redeploy the prior snapshot edge fn (v10 body, drop the claim/release + call-site wrapper); `DROP FUNCTION public.claim_pipeline_lock(text,integer); DROP FUNCTION public.release_pipeline_lock(text); DROP TABLE public.pipeline_run_locks;`; delete the 2 workflow files.

- **SHIPPED + VERIFIED LIVE — `pack-events-ingest` forward wedge fixed by PER-CHUNK commit (final deploy Version `d9f863b5-ff7a-4e1f-a193-d04a015875d5`, commit `80a9238`).** Two fixes; the first was insufficient, caught by Trevor running a real cron test + `wrangler tail`:
  - **First attempt (`fdb09b59`) — staged sub-budgets — DID NOT WORK.** Theory: live legs (`purchases → opens → allday_forward`) share one 20s budget and purchases (perpetually chunk-capped since the ~19:00Z 06-25 pack drop, 4,938 primary_withdraw rows) starved the rest. Gave purchases 12s / opens 16s / allday the tail. **Disproven by observation:** budgets are measured absolute-from-`startedMs`, but each leg's FLUSH runs *between* the loop sections, so the purchases flush ate the opens budget window → opens still `chunks:0`. AND the real failure mode was worse — all cursor writes + the `pipeline_runs` log are batched at the very END, so a run killed by the CF wall-clock/CPU limit committed **NOTHING** (no `pipeline_runs` row since 22:24Z; `wrangler tail` showed live `POST /` invocations dying before the end). "Timing out and committing nothing."
  - **Real fix (`80a9238`) — per-chunk flush + cursor commit.** `processCursor` now takes an optional `commitChunk(toBlock)` callback; the 3 live legs flush their accumulated batch + `writeCursor` + clear the accumulator **after EACH ~250-block chunk** (instead of one batched flush + cursor-write at the end). Every chunk is durably banked → a hot-drop backlog **self-drains** over runs, and a killed run keeps all finished chunks. Moving the flush inside the loop also makes the staged budget guards finally effective (they now include flush time). Backfill mode untouched (keeps its single end-of-leg flush). tsc clean.
  - **VERIFIED LIVE (2 cron runs, both `ok=true`, ~20s, logged):** the two 554-min-frozen cursors UNFROZE — `topshot_pack_opens` 156004422→156005672 (3+2 chunks), `allday_pack_purchases` 156004422→156007922 (9+5 chunks), `topshot_pack_purchases` 156009044→156017794 (16+19 chunks). ### 2026-07-08 (Cowork interactive) — concierge check_wallet told the truth-free version of a $82k portfolio; fixed end-to-end (eeff0b1 + 187669e, verified live)

Trevor's first real Telegram DM QA exposed three data bugs in the concierge wallet path (bot reported "24 moments / $146.77" for a wallet actually holding **18,796 moments / ~$82.4k**; "UFC" answers mirrored Top Shot; AllDay errored with $0). All fixed + live-verified via the trusted-bot smoke path (`tg:cowork-smoke-4`: full correct rundown, one tool call; smoke rows flagged).

- **`eeff0b1` — wallet-search unknown-slug fall-through (the UFC-mirrors-TS bug):** any unrecognized collectionId (e.g. `ufc-strike`, which the model naturally invents) silently fell through to the Top Shot on-chain walk, returning TS moments labeled as the other collection. Now: aliases normalized (underscores/bare names/ufc-strike) and unknown slugs 400 with the valid list. Applies to ALL callers of /api/wallet-search.
- **`eeff0b1` — check_wallet reads `get_wallet_collection_snapshot` first** (the /share RPC; 222ms warm): full-wallet totals + per-collection breakdown + top-5 + rarest + badge count across all 5 collections in ONE call — replaces the old path that walked the chain and reported a single 24-row page (limit default) as the portfolio, and enriched AllDay ids through TS GQL (guaranteed `Unknown (error loading)` + $0). Optional collectionId adds that collection's wmc top-5. Unindexed wallets fall back to the live TS walk with `summary.totalMoments` + an explicit page-only note. Tool schema now enumerates the exact 5 slugs and instructs ONE call for cross-collection questions.
- **`187669e` — per-tool timeout budget:** the blanket 6s Promise.race killed check_wallet twice at exactly 6000ms during the known contention window (trace 04:27Z) even though the snapshot RPC is 222ms warm. Wallet tools (`check_wallet`, `check_wallet_squeeze`) now get 20s; all other tools keep 6s. This was also the cause of Trevor's very first "Wallet lookup timed out" DM on the OLD code (12s inner fetch under a 6s race = guaranteed loss under load).
- **Revert:** `git revert 187669e eeff0b1` (code-only, no migrations). Smoke rows: `DELETE FROM support_conversations WHERE session_id LIKE 'tg:cowork-smoke-%';`
- **Note for the night pass:** wallet-search's AllDay leg still enriches via TS GQL/Cadence internally (pre-existing; concierge no longer depends on it for indexed wallets). If a real web consumer hits it, the durable fix is wmc-based assembly for the AllDay branch — CC lane, not auto-shippable.

### 2026-07-07 (Cowork interactive) — conversational concierge over Telegram + Discord DM (d07ed2f + a30c790, deploys READY; /ask registered)

Trevor: make the bot concierge conversational ("white glove — meet users where they are"), not command-only. Diagnosed + shipped end-to-end; smoke-verified live on prod (session `tg:cowork-smoke-2`: identity "I've got you, jamesdillonbond" + memory recall across turns; rows flagged is_smoke_test).

- **Root finds:** (1) bot DMs had NO memory — support-chat only replays client-passed `conversationHistory` (web widget); the bridge never sends it. (2) **Regression:** the cookie-only `deriveIdentity()` hardening silently killed the 06-17 `b00b914` DM personalization — the bridge's body `ownerKey` was dropped, every bot DM answered anonymous. (3) Discord had no conversational path at all (Interactions webhook = slash-commands only). (4) Telegram replies showed raw markdown, no typing indicator.
- **support-chat (`d07ed2f` + `a30c790`):** `isTrustedBotRequest` — `x-rpc-bot-secret` header, timing-safe vs INGEST_SECRET_TOKEN **or CRON_SECRET** (a30c790; the two are treated as equivalent everywhere else — the INGEST-only first cut no-op'd for CRON-keyed callers, caught by live smoke). When trusted + `pageContext='bot_dm'`: accepts bridge-resolved `ownerKey` (+ allow_list wallet lookup) and rebuilds the last 8 turns from `support_conversations` by session_id (`loadBotDmHistory`). New bot_dm system-prompt branch: plain text, chat-length, bare URLs, conversational follow-ups.
- **concierge-bridge:** sends the trusted-bot header (INGEST).
- **Telegram:** sendChatAction typing indicator; markdown→plain-text sanitizer (`toTelegramPlain`); 4096-char split; concierge-aware /help; maxDuration 60.
- **Discord:** new `/ask question:` slash command → concierge (deferred reply, `dc:<userId>` session, linked-handle personalization, 2000-char cap); maxDuration 60. **Registered globally** via POST /api/bots/discord/register → `["link","soldpacks","alerts","ask"]` (global propagation to DMs ≤ ~1h).
- **OPERATOR (Trevor):** confirm `ALERTS_BOT_CONCIERGE=1` is set in Vercel prod env — it gates BOTH the Telegram free-text branch and `/ask` (not readable from Cowork; everything else is verified live). Also: `.env.production`'s INGEST_SECRET_TOKEN (May 12) is STALE — it now matches live CRON_SECRET, not INGEST; worth refreshing the local file on next rotation pass.
- **Revert:** `git revert a30c790 d07ed2f` (code-only; no migrations). Smoke rows: `DELETE FROM support_conversations WHERE session_id LIKE 'tg:cowork-smoke-%';`

### 2026-06-27 (Cowork) — cron→GHA decouple part 2: guard-then-backstop CC prompt (supersedes the "atomic cutover" framing)
**✅ RESOLVED 2026-06-27 (CC `611b2fb`) + 06-30 — DONE; stop carrying as queued.** The guard-then-backstop shipped: the `pipeline_run_locks` concurrency guard + `wallet-backfill-backstop.yml` + `snapshot-institutional-wallets-backstop.yml` GHA workflows are live (verified 2026-06-30 — both workflow files present in `.github/workflows/`); the sales-indexer backstop was extended to golazos/ufc on 06-30. Reliability goal met. Only OPTIONAL operator twin-disables remain, and those are deliberately NOT recommended — the cron-job.org primaries are intentional dual-fire redundancy (the guard makes an overlapping run a cheap no-op), so disabling them removes a safety net for no gain.
The remaining cron-job.org-only writer families (`snapshot-institutional-wallets` + the `seed-wallet-refresh`/`wallet-backfill` cohort family). Investigation CORRECTED the premise: both are row-level idempotent (UPSERT on unique keys / EXISTS-guarded) — double-firing does NOT dup rows; the real cost is 2× on-chain Cadence + DBSAT pool saturation. So the fix is **concurrency-guard (advisory lock / claim-row, no-op on overlap) THEN a redundant GHA backstop** — NOT an atomic cutover. With the guard, cron-job.org can stay (harmless) or be disabled later as optional operator cleanup. Family #2 guard is the priority (only place overlap is expensive); #1 is belt-and-suspenders (read-only). Full prompt with file:line anchors + guard patterns (`allow_list_claim_prewarm`, `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock`): **docs/cc-prompt-wallet-snapshot-guard-backstop.md**.

### 2026-06-27 (Cowork) — AllDay unmapped: REAL root cause + CC prompt (supersedes the "classify as residual" lean)
AllDay unmapped_sales = **576 open priced** (29 recent ≤14d + 547 old Apr-8 backfill). Root cause is NOT the consumer-GQL 403 and NOT price — it is **stale holder attribution**: the existing `allday-resolve-unmapped` Leg B borrows editionID on-chain against the sale-time buyer, but AllDay moments leave the buyer ~9min (~439 blocks) post-sale, so `borrowMomentNFT` returns nil (onchain_nil≈60/tick, promoted 0). FIX = forward `AllDay.Deposit` scan from `block_height` → current holder → borrow (reuses `BORROW_MOMENT_SCRIPT` + `resolve_unmapped_sales_for_collection`; Flow REST is WAF-proof). Drains the 29 recent + STOPS forward accumulation (the bigger win). 547 old = optional one-shot bulk Deposit walk, or leave (quarantined, ~2% AllDay). CC prompt: **docs/cc-prompt-allday-unmapped-resolution.md**.

NEW finding (measured tonight): it is **necessary but INSUFFICIENT** — I timed the refresh through the exact fn the cron calls: `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY topshot_special_serial_owners_mv` ran **>135s** (still active at 135s, completed before the 180s fn-timeout), which **exceeds the route's `maxDuration=120`** (the route runs the refresh in `after()`). So at the next cron tick the lambda is killed at 120s BEFORE the refresh completes AND before `log_pipeline_run` → the cron now produces **NO pipeline_runs entry (silent failure)** instead of the prior logged ok=false. Root cause: the MV's base view full-scans TS `wallet_moments_cache` (~70s) and CONCURRENTLY ~doubles it (135–160s); the sibling it was modeled on (`refresh-pack-grail-metrics-mv`, maxDuration 60) works only because its MV is tiny/fast. **Fix (one of):** (A) CC route+deploy — raise `maxDuration` 120→~200 in `app/api/cron/refresh-special-serial-owners-mv/route.ts` (keeps CONCURRENTLY) + bump fn `statement_timeout` 180→~210s for margin (`ALTER FUNCTION public.refresh_topshot_special_serial_owners_mv() SET statement_timeout='210s';`); OR (B) DB-only but CC's design call — change the fn body `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY` → plain `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW` (~70–90s, fits maxDuration=120, brief AccessExclusive lock — fine on a low-cadence noindex board, but reverses CC's deliberate CONCURRENTLY choice). NOT night-pass-shipped: route hot file (`93ff06c` ~8h old); option B reverses an explicit CC design decision; LOW blast radius (board serves its snapshot, **manually refreshed tonight** → 5,929 rows fresh). Revert of the live 04:38Z fix (if ever needed): `ALTER FUNCTION ... SET statement_timeout='30s';` (leave it — correct, just insufficient alone).

- **ASK-ONLY-CAP · ✅ DECIDED 2026-06-11 (CC review, Trevor in the loop) — DO NOT SHIP the ASK_ONLY cohort cap; do NOT re-propose it.** The k=3 × set+tier-median cap FAILS its own acceptance test on the LiveToken-matched cohort (median 1.113→0.559, severe-low 0→54, fixes only 4/6 severe-high; 124/199 capped). ASK_ONLY editions are selected-for-illiquidity rarer parallels worth far above the set median, so a set-median cap guts grails; the headline "offender" (Booker Cosmic 8:110, RPC $6,750) is legit (own sales median $2,214/max $8,422, recent) and capping conflicts with the deliberate `dampenGrailSpike` high-tier carve-out. Full disproof: `docs/proposals/ask-only-sanity-cap-2026-06-11.md` + memory `ask-only-is-a-sales-ingest-gap`.
  **The real root cause it uncovered — TS-SALES-INGEST-GAP:** ASK_ONLY ≈ the bucket of editions whose sales we never captured. Across the 989-row LiveToken crosscheck, share with 0 sales in our DB: ASK_ONLY 68% (115/169) vs LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH ~0%. Live TS ASK_ONLY: 785/1005 (78%) have 0 sales. Trevor's own Grant 103:3792 buys ($67/$79) live in `moment_acquisitions` but never reached `sales`.
  **Phase 1 (moment_acquisitions promotion) — TRIED + REVERTED 2026-06-11 (Trevor-authorized w/ guards; FAILED acceptance).** Shipped `audit_20260611_promote_ask_only_acquisition_sales_v1` (185 rows / 134 editions, tagged `acq_promotion_v1`, absolute $50k fat-finger ceiling — the set-tier-relative cut deliberately omitted as it over-excludes legit star/low-serial premiums). LiveToken acceptance re-run FAILED: matched (41 ed) median abs-err 0.363→0.401, severe-high 1→4; recency-restricting didn't help (n=8, 0.965). Root cause: a single recorded *purchase* price (old peak-era buy / one idiosyncratic deal) is NOISIER than the *current ask*, which already tracks LiveToken (old ASK_ONLY median ratio 0.984). Premise "a real buy beats a lone ask" DISPROVEN. **Reverted:** `DELETE FROM public.sales WHERE source='acq_promotion_v1';` (0 rows remain; FMV input at baseline). Three wrong fixes (global 0.75 cut, set-median cap, acquisitions promotion) all caught by the LiveToken gate.
  **Phase 2 (the real path, NOT auto-shippable — FMV-feeding pipeline, needs a build):** per-edition real MARKET sales backfill from TS `searchMarketplaceTransactions` (consensus of many txns, LiveToken-grade — qualitatively better than one purchase), paced cron scoped to the ASK_ONLY set, with the UUID-vs-int setID resolution detail. Gated on its own LiveToken acceptance. Writeup: `docs/proposals/ts-sales-ingest-gap-backfill-2026-06-11.md`. Companion items: PIN-FMV-REKEY-WAVES 2/3 parked; PACKVIZ-GRID ✅ CLOSED (shipped `41dfae2` — see its own entry below); `audit_lt_matches` KEEP.

- **OFFER-SANITY-RAISE · 2026-06-09 (data-quality sweep, recommend) · [LOW-MED · CC — edition_offers writer, off-limits to the passes] schedule the durable GREATEST-based `edition_offers.highest_offer` raise.** `v_offer_sanity_flags` = 174 (was 132 on 06-07); composition stable + structural: 138 (79%) are `chain_exceeds_gql`+sub_serial (GQL collapses subedition/serial offers; max gap $658), 21 `gql_blank_chain_has` (the 12 non-subedition ones carry the headline gaps — SGA Kingmaker $5,222, LeBron Supernova $5,000, Wilt $1,501). Growth tracks on-chain offer accrual, not a fault. Durable fix (per `rpc-offers-data-model` memory): raise `edition_offers.highest_offer` via `GREATEST(existing, chain_open_max)` — never clobber down — keyed off the on-chain `offers` open-max, in a CC-owned job; recurring raise (slowly grows), not a one-time backfill. Optional companion: add `offer_sanity_max_gap` to `v_rpc_trust_health` scoped to edition-grain gaps (CREATE OR REPLACE on the live monitoring view — CC, not a watchlist row). Sweep did NOT write edition_offers (task-forbidden). **CLASSIFICATION LOCKED 2026-06-09 (Trevor):** `offers.offer_type` is 3 nested grains, each displays at its OWN grain — **edition** (universal across the edition), **subedition** (that subedition only), **serial** (that one serial only). The edition "Best offer" cell may be raised ONLY by `offer_type='edition'` (a serial/subedition bid is not the edition floor). Open volumes: edition 5,284 / subedition 1,541 / serial 535. Validated raise scope = `GREATEST(existing, max(offers WHERE offer_type='edition' AND status='open'))`: the REAL edition-cell gap is just **14 editions** (`gql_blank_chain_has`, ~$17.2k total, max $5,222 SGA Kingmaker / $5,000 LeBron Supernova / $1,501 Wilt); the other 13 edition-grain flags are penny noise ($14 total) and the 147 sub/serial flags are correctly out-of-scope. Small + safe. Subedition offers → a subedition-grain surface; serial offers → that serial's moment view. Detail: `docs/data-quality-sweep-2026-06-09.md`.
  **CC investigation 2026-06-12 (did NOT ship — two reasons, both needing a call above CC):** (1) **The raise must be edition-level, NOT a blind `GREATEST(existing, chain_open_max)`.** `v_offer_sanity_flags.chain_max_offer` is `max(offer_amount_usd)` over ALL open offers incl. `offer_type IN ('subedition','serial')`, and `has_sub_serial` is `bool_or` across the edition — so 79% of flags are serial-locked offers (a bid for serial #1 only). Raising an *edition*-level "Best offer" display to a serial-locked offer would misrepresent the price. The correct raise filters to `offer_type NOT IN ('subedition','serial')`: measured live = **30 editions** have a genuine edition-level on-chain offer above `edition_offers.highest_offer` (max gap still $5,222 — SGA Kingmaker), the real actionable set. That's a pricing-display semantics + recurring-cron decision = Trevor's product call + operator wiring, not auto-shippable. (2) **The `offer_sanity_max_gap` monitor must land WITH the raise, not before it.** Adding it to `v_rpc_trust_health` now would BREACH immediately on the $5,222 gap and stay breached until the (unbuilt) raise job closes it = chronic paging noise — the opposite of `fmv_sanity_flags` (which breaches at 1 because those are fixable now). - **PINFMV-DRIFT-14 · RESOLVED 2026-06-10 (CC).** Root cause was NOT an FMV leak in the concierge route — it was the **smoke drift guard's comparison-set truncation**. The guard (`app/api/smoke-test/route.ts`) built its valid-triple Set with `.from("pinnacle_catalog")...limit(5000)`, which PostgREST clamps to the 1,000-row server max; with 1,806 priced renders (counted 2026-06-10) ~806 renders silently fell out of the comparison set every tick (stable scan order = same renders dropped), so any deal whose render lived in the truncated tail false-flagged as a "leak" → deterministic re-trip. `searchPinnacleDeals` was ALREADY render-keyed since `a9f86af` (reads `floor_ask` + `fmv_usd` from the same `pinnacle_catalog` row, verified by direct read of `lib/concierge/pinnacle-router.ts` L115-174) — the route needed no change, and the queued `render_id`-keying work item is **MOOT (struck)**. Fix: bounded the guard's catalog fetch to `.in("character_name", distinctPlayersUnderTest)` (each character has <1,000 renders, clamp can't truncate) + an inconclusive-on-clamp-ceiling guard. SQL-simulated against the full priced set pre-ship: zero real leaks (every current Goofy deal triple present). `NEXTJS-14` to resolve with regression arming after one clean tick. Revert: `git revert <commit>` (guard returns to the truncated fetch; harmless but noisy).

- **Q4 · RESOLVED 2026-05-31 (Claude Code, commit `48f5a98`, deploy `dpl_Gaz5NMzE2JTpViRPHZoQjKwJ9pKx` READY).** Root cause was NOT a counting bug — it was a wrong-table resolver. Both `pinnacle-listings-indexer` and `-retry` derived an `edition_key` then looked it up ONLY in `editions` (whose Pinnacle coverage is ~0; live: 0 of 104 distinct failing keys were in `editions`), queuing a failure on every miss → genuinely-new failures every tick → the warn fired honestly each tick. But `cached_listings_v2.edition_id` is a UUID FK to `editions.id`, so Pinnacle (editions live in `pinnacle_editions`) could NEVER populate it — the whole failure/retry machinery chased an impossible target.
  Fix: both routes now resolve the edition_key against `pinnacle_editions` AND `editions`; a hit in either = resolved (no failure / mark resolved), `edition_id` stays null for the pinnacle_editions-only case (expected, by design). Miss reason renamed `edition_key_not_in_editions_table` → `edition_key_unmapped` (key in neither table = genuinely unknown edition). Sentry capture rate/reason-gated: pages only on a real spike (>25 new/tick) OR an unexpected reason, not per-tick. Scoped to the two Pinnacle-only routes; AllDay indexer (editions-backed, legit reason) untouched. One-time DML stamped 224 now-resolvable capped-backlog rows resolved (945 → 719 unresolved, the genuinely-unmappable tail). Live: 73 of 104 distinct mapped keys are in `pinnacle_editions`.
  Revert: `git revert 48f5a98` (code only; the resolved_at stamp is harmless to leave).
  Target: `NEXTJS-15` stops firing every tick (mark Sentry issue resolved after 24h quiet); new `listing_resolution_failures` inserts trend toward the genuinely-unmappable residual only.

- **Q8 · 2026-05-31 (PM, Claude Code) · [MED] badge-sync upsert poisons ~40% of batches → coverage barely grows.**
  SHIPPED (CC, commit `bd8f663`, deploy `dpl_5J9aTFbz4vG85LusJP4J7a5Pe7TS` READY): the badge-sync sweep was capped at MAX_PAGES(20)×100 = 2,000 editions/badge; raised to 80 (+`maxDuration=300`, PAGE_DELAY 400→250). Verified the cap is genuinely lifted — Top Shot Debut sweep now returns 2,018 (>old 2,000) and Rookie Year 1,138→1,596 distinct. BUT a full sweep grew the table only +23 (3,012→3,035 TS rows): the `POST /api/badge-sync` run logged `upserted:2006, upsertErrors:28` — ~1,400 collected rows dropped. ROOT CAUSE: the upsert uses `onConflict:"id"` but the table also has `badge_editions_ext_coll_unique UNIQUE(external_id,collection_id)`; parallels of the same play share an `external_id` but differ in `id`, so a parallel with a new id takes INSERT → violates the unique constraint → fails the WHOLE 50-row supabase-js batch. The lifted cap surfaces MORE parallels → more poisoned batches. Existing rows are a mix (2,819 parallel_id=0 + ~216 non-zero) so the table's row-grain is itself ambiguous (PK embeds parallel; unique key collapses to one-per-play). FIX (needs a row-grain decision + Trevor review — not rushed): decide one-row-per-play vs per-parallel, align `onConflict` + the unique constraint, and dedup the sweep by `external_id` in code to avoid "ON CONFLICT … cannot affect row a second time". NOT auto-shippable (touches the badge write path + a constraint). Memory: badge-editions-key-format-and-upsert-poison.
  SEPARATE finding (Item 4 verify): the 9 LiveToken-flagged classics (Harden Holo MMXX, Wemby/SGA/Giannis Metallic Gold LE, Dame Cosmic, Caitlin Clark WNBA, Sabonis Run It Back, KD Supernova; Stephon Castle gets DERIVED badges only) still return `badges:[]`

The handoff's "0/1,572, reads frozen cached_listings" premise was stale.

- **#5 Pinnacle Pack EV — INVESTIGATED end-to-end, NOT shipped (by design). The handoff's uniform-EV method is DISPROVEN by measurement.** Full finding: `docs/handoff-2026-07-01-pinnacle-pack-ev-measured-finding.md`. Confirmed the odds source works (`searchDistributions` GQL, studio-platform, no secret; Pinnacle typename `A.edf9df96c92f4595.PackNFT.NFT`; `editionIds`→`pinnacle_catalog.edition_id` 1:1; worked example reproduces exactly). BUT: Pinnacle has had **one pack drop ever** ("Summer Splash", 8 unique distributions, mostly sold out), `packOdds` is `[]` on all of them, and the "distributions" are **rarity facets of a single pack, not buyable packs**. The handoff's proposed **uniform average → GARBAGE**: $4.99 pack showing EV $2,651 (**531× value_ratio**), 188×, 111× — actively-misleading fake deals. Measured the correct alternative live: **supply-weighted odds reconstruction** (`P(facet) ∝ facet total_supply`, parent-grouped) → $4.99 Standard pack EV ≈ **$27.87 (5.6×)**, sane. That's a genuine pricing model (parent grouping + odds inference) = review-gated per the handoff, on a thin one-drop surface → **queued for Trevor, not autonomously shipped**. Do NOT wire a uniform-EV Pinnacle pack surface.

Attended close-out of the audit + a full sweep of every scheduled-task output (monitor inbox, nightly queued registry, focus.md, the 20 enabled tasks). Platform GREEN (security 0/0/0/0, trust 15/15, prod deploy READY). 1 route commit (`795d99b`, deploy `dpl_HLe4cww` READY).

- **SHIPPED — classify-acquisitions AllDay `p_limit` 300→80 (`795d99b`) — RESOLVES CLASSIFY-ACQ-ALLDAY-STATEMENT-TIMEOUT (the sole item the 07-01 overnight queued).** The `nfl_all_day` leg of `classify-acquisitions-multicollection` flapped (~40% of hourly ticks) at `backfill_acquisitions_for_collection`'s 90s `statement_timeout`. Measured the candidate CTE read-only (EXPLAIN ANALYZE): a Merge-Anti-Join over the FULL AllDay sales history (all partitions, sorted by nft_id) probing the sparse wmc cache, so cost scales ~linearly with the batch — **LIMIT 150 = 67.6s** (against the 90s cap → the flap), **LIMIT 80 = 36.3s**. This DISPROVES the overnight's suggested `p_limit`→120 (still ~54s, too hot under load); 80 gives ~2.5x headroom so every tick completes green and the 3-collection `after()` loop stays under `maxDuration=120`. No logic/semantics change (same rows classified, fewer per tick); non-user-facing moment_acquisitions enrichment. **Revert:** set the `nfl_all_day` target `limit` back to `300` in `app/api/cron/classify-acquisitions-multicollection/route.ts`. **Target:** next hourly tick logs ok=true.
- **RESOLVED (accept posture) — DORMANT-ANON-DML-REVOKE-REGRESSED (monitor Candidate 1, 07-01) — verified NOT a live hole.** Independently re-measured: **154** public base tables carry the dormant Supabase-default anon INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE grant, but **anon_write_rls_off=0** and **anon_write_reachable_via_policy=0** — every grant is unreachable (RLS on + zero permissive public/anon write policies → writes default-denied), which is why `check_public_security_invariants()`=[] and `anon_write_holes`=[]. The grants re-accumulate because Supabase re-applies schema-wide defaults on platform ops, so a one-time REVOKE is non-durable AND a 154-table sweep (incl. hot editions/wmc/sales partitions) carries real DDL risk. **Disposition: ACCEPT the RLS-on posture as the security boundary** — grant-revocation is redundant when RLS is on with no permissive write policy. Not a hole; do NOT re-run a broad REVOKE and do NOT re-flag the grant-count drift as an incident (the live-hole class = RLS-OFF is already covered by the invariant checks).
- **RESOLVED (adequately handled) — TEAM-PAGE-STATEMENT-TIMEOUT (monitor Candidate 2, 07-01).** Measured `get_team_detail` on the heaviest TS team (Lakers, 580 editions): **1,747ms cold** — well under its existing 8s `statement_timeout`; `get_team_activity` ~187ms warm (its LATERAL rewrite was measured-and-disproven earlier this session — plpgsql variable-array plan trap, 18s regression). Both RPCs already fail-fast at 8s → degrade to an empty section (graceful). The rare team-page timeouts (count 8, 2–4 users, long-standing since 06-04, NOT a regression) are connection-pool-saturation collateral — the same class the 07-01 edition/pack/player perf wave is already shrinking + the operator `db-pool` bump addresses — not a team-RPC algorithmic problem. No team-specific fix warranted (LATERAL disproven; a `sales(edition_id,sold_at)` composite would tax the ingest hot path for a 1.75s query). Do NOT re-flag as a fresh perf item.

## 2026-07-05

### audit_20260705_widen_pack_ev_value_ratio_full
- **What:** Widened `pack_ev_history.value_ratio` NUMERIC(6,3) → NUMERIC(12,4). Dropped and recreated all 15 dependent views/MVs atomically in a single transaction.
- **Why:** `numeric field overflow` Postgres ERROR firing in bursts since 5:13 AM PT. Root cause: packs with very low price but high constituent FMV produce `gross_ev / pack_price > 999.999`, exceeding the old column max. `topshot_pack_reality_top_ev` was stuck at 7 rows (only the packs under 999.999 succeeded).
- **Verified:** Column confirmed NUMERIC(12,4) via `information_schema.columns`. All 3 MV indexes recreated. All 9 security_invoker=on views confirmed. Pack EV board will repopulate automatically on next cron-job.org pipeline tick.
- **Revert:** Rerun the same drop/alter/recreate cycle with NUMERIC(6,3) — this restores the overflow bug, so revert is only meaningful if a data corruption issue surfaces.

### audit_20260705_stagger_job38_away_from_fmv_recalc

- **Why:** `*/15` ticks at :00/:15/:30/:45 caused DB contention with fmv-recalc peaks at :08/:28/:48 (`refresh_wmc_fmv_drift_active` runs 77s). New schedule puts all four ticks at least 43s clear of all three fmv windows.
- **Verified:** `SELECT schedule FROM cron.job WHERE jobid = 38` returns `5,20,35,50 * * * *`.
- **Revert:** `SELECT cron.alter_job(38, '*/15 * * * *');` via `execute_sql` or `apply_migration`.

### audit_20260705_stagger_job35_mv_pack_ev_refresh
- **What:** Rescheduled pg_cron job 35 (`rpc-refresh-mv-pack-ev-latest`) from `*/10` to `3,13,23,33,43,53 * * * *` via `cron.alter_job(35, '3,13,23,33,43,53 * * * *')`.
- **Why:** `*/10` ticks at :10/:30/:50 fell 43s after fmv-recalc peaks at :08/:28/:48. New schedule puts all 6 ticks symmetrically — 5min before or 3.7min after each fmv window. Function is pure `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY mv_pack_ev_latest`, not pricing-adjacent.
- **Verified:** `SELECT schedule FROM cron.job WHERE jobid = 35` returns `3,13,23,33,43,53 * * * *`.
- **Revert:** `SELECT cron.alter_job(35, '*/10 * * * *');`

### audit_20260705_stagger_job27_allday_dist_opened
- **What:** Rescheduled pg_cron job 27 (`rpc-allday-dist-opened-backfill`) from `*/4` to `2-58/4 * * * *` via `cron.alter_job(27, '2-58/4 * * * *')`.
- **Why:** `*/4` fires at :00/:04/:08/:12/... — the :08/:28/:48 ticks land directly on fmv-recalc peaks. Shifting by 2 minutes gives a 2-min buffer before each peak. Not pricing-adjacent: function is `backfill-allday-dist-opened` HTTP call (data ingestion only).

- **Revert:** `SELECT cron.alter_job(27, '*/4 * * * *');`

### audit_20260705_fix_set152_honors_diced_tier
- **What:** Set `tier = 'RARE'` on 22 NULL-tier editions in "2023-24 Honors (Diced)" (set 152, TopShot).
- **Why:** All 25 editions in the set share `edition_kind=LE`, `series=6`, `circulation_count=10`. Three siblings (Giannis, Tatum, Luka) already had `tier=RARE` — the remaining 22 (Steph Curry, SGA, Jokić, etc.) had NULL tier due to an ingest gap. 
- **Verified:** `SELECT tier, COUNT(*) FROM editions WHERE external_id::text LIKE '152:%' AND external_id::text ~ '^[0-9]+:[0-9]+$' AND collection_id = '95f28a17-...' GROUP BY tier` → `RARE: 25`, no NULLs.
- **Revert:** `UPDATE editions SET tier = NULL WHERE collection_id = '95f28a17-224a-4025-96ad-adf8a4c63bfd' AND external_id::text LIKE '152:%' AND external_id::text ~ '^[0-9]+:[0-9]+$' AND tier = 'RARE' AND set_name = '2023-24 Honors (Diced)' AND edition_kind = 'LE'` (restores the data gap).

- rpc-refresh-special-serial-owners-mv was the lone straggler left on postgres/120s when the 07-12 cron_heavy migration moved its 15 siblings; it timed out its 04:13Z tick. cron.alter_job(username:=) needs superuser (Supabase postgres is not), so moved it via GRANT EXECUTE on the SECDEF refresh fn + SET ROLE cron_heavy + cron.schedule() (schedule keys per-user, so it created new job 109 as cron_heavy; unscheduled the old postgres job 10). Now single job 109, cron_heavy (600s), 13 4,16. Revert: reschedule the name as postgres + REVOKE.

- rpc-refresh-misattrib-candidates (already cron_heavy/600s) timed out its ONLY daily tick at 03:25Z (overnight-backfill peak). EXPLAIN: the refresh seq-scans the full Top Shot sales history on set_a + set_b (~613k cost; Top Shot is the dominant collection so collection_id indexes are not selective and go unused; the purpose-built idx_sales_*_ts_edserial_collide indexes are used + pristine). So NOT a REINDEX case -- it is contention. Rescheduled 03:25Z -> 15:35Z (quiet window, clears overnight 00-06Z + evening 21-01Z peaks + fmv :08/:28/:48). Internal helper MV; timing not user-visible. Revert: back to 25 3.

### FINDING (not fixed; deferred to off-peak maintenance) -- collection_id_sold_at index bloat
- sales_YYYY_collection_id_sold_at_idx across partitions are 61-71% leaf-fragmented (~65% density), from out-of-order backfill inserts (the collide partial indexes are pristine at 90%/0%). NOT the misattrib bottleneck (that query seq-scans and never touches them), but they serve selective-collection + collection+sold_at range queries platform-wide. Recommend an off-peak REINDEX CONCURRENTLY sweep across the 7 sales partitions. Deliberately not run now (evening contention window would add I/O).

### INFRA -- desktop Cowork push-cred injection is ABSENT and INDEPENDENT of bash recovery
- The desktop sandbox gets NO push-cred injection: no credential helper, no github.com->auth-proxy insteadOf rewrite, no GITHUB_TOKEN/GH_TOKEN/PAT env, no gh, no .git-credentials/.netrc -- unlike web Cowork. Push works ONLY by copying the mount clone tokenized remote.origin.pushurl into the working clone. Verified 07-13 via a real push auth negotiation (accepted). The two sandbox failure modes (useradd/no-space vs push-cred) are INDEPENDENT -- bash-green does NOT imply push-green. Provisioning should inject the same auth-proxy rewrite the web container gets. This ledger commit is itself the end-to-end push proof.
